### Owner decision note — 2026-10-17 13:30 UTC (consolidated; nothing below is pre-empted)

**0. Continue-on-error rule (explicit resolution, owner instruction ≈13:18 UTC).** Diagnostic steps may keep collecting evidence; every required failure must still fail the final acceptance gate. What the J candidate workflow enforces today: the only `continue-on-error: true` is the `Terraform Format Check` diagnostic (`terraform-validate` job, `ci.yml` ≈510); the `Terraform Validate` step that follows has no such flag and fails the job; every other `if: always()` step is a summary/artifact upload that cannot change a job's conclusion; `release-gate` `needs` all eight jobs (`ui-check`, `check`, `postgres-integration`, `evaluation-harness`, `container-build`, `k8s-validate`, `terraform-validate`, `backup-restore-drill`, `ci.yml` ≈628–641), so any failed required job leaves the gate skipped, never green; the required C2/C3 database step and the 21-module step propagate pytest's exit status through the PIPESTATUS pattern; the J reservation rule stays "never add continue-on-error to a required step". The independent Opus re-review of J-02 (13:28 UTC) confirmed that no required failure can produce a green `check`, `postgres-integration` or `container-build` job today, and named four gaps that are now J-03 corrections, not waivers: the `release-gate` job is SKIPPED rather than FAILED after a required failure (it must run under `if: always()` and fail unless all eight results are success), the `k8s-validate` kubeconform pipeline masks its exit code behind `tee`, the terraform validate summary line is printed as passed regardless of outcome, and neither required PostgreSQL invocation asserts zero skipped cases. Acceptance itself is the owner's green-before-next-task rule over the exact published head, not any single job.

**1. Runner pin — Options A and B (recovery evidence; no choice assumed; frozen pin unchanged).** Facts: the frozen wrapper `scripts/run_task8_v27_signed_census.py` pins `_APPROVED_SQL_RUNNER_SHA256 = d12ea370…` and `tests/slice100_task8/test_run_task8_v27_signed_census.py::test_v27_cli_pins_the_frozen_sql_runner_bytes` asserts the on-disk `scripts/run_task8_v27_sql_authority_census.py` hashes to it; the on-disk runner is the later security-hardened `812bfeb2…` (verified again 13:30 UTC), so that required test is RED at every current head. The reconstruction, ledger (162 candidates), scripts and SHA256SUMS are preserved under `R/runner-recovery-20261017/` (`RECOVERED-run_task8_v27_sql_authority_census.py`). It is a provenance-documented reconstruction, not a recovered original artifact, and the historical-source route must say so. — **Option A** (keep the frozen pin; use the reconstructed bytes only for isolated, explicitly labelled historical-source verification of the signed-census harness, never as current runtime): historical verification becomes possible with exact byte identity and documented provenance; current acceptance is unchanged — the hardened runner stays current and the pin test stays a required RED, so A alone cannot make CI green. — **Option B** (separately authorize a reviewed re-pin of the wrapper and its pin test to the hardened `812bfeb2…` runner with a new signed receipt): the pin test turns green once the new receipt exists; it is a frozen-pin change outside current authority, needs independent Opus review of the exact delta, and the new signed census receipt can only be produced by the governance signer with the private signing key the owner holds (under `C:\IDIS-secrets`, never used by Fable); historical verification is unaffected if A is applied as well. — **Fable's recommendation:** A now, and B as a separately authorized, reviewed, bounded change (wrapper constant, pin test, new receipt) because without B the required RED is permanent. A and B are not mutually exclusive. The owner decides; nothing has been changed.

**2. Recovery credential — type, minimum access, destination, entry, and qualification status.** Mechanism actually implemented (`scripts/ci_private_inputs.py` `fetch_private`, lines ≈841–928): the secret is written to an invocation-private 0600 file and used only as an SSH identity (`ssh -i … -o IdentitiesOnly=yes -o BatchMode=yes -o StrictHostKeyChecking=yes`, host key pinned to github.com's ed25519 key in the script); git performs one `fetch --no-tags --depth=1` of commit `1a094b854c2e8766ba4516ff6edd1a4023ec1171` from `git@github.com:albarami/IDIS-private-recovery.git` into a bare transport repository, verifies the tree `48902779…`, the two manifest blobs by pinned SHA-256 and every allowed artifact by hash, stages them under the 0700 invocation root, and deletes the key file and transport tree afterwards. **Credential type:** an SSH private key whose public half is registered as a **read-only deploy key** on the private repository `albarami/IDIS-private-recovery` — the least privilege the mechanism can use (repository-scoped, read-only, no account token). **Destination:** environment secret `IDIS_RECOVERY_READ_KEY` on environment `cir04-private-inputs` of `albarami/IDIS` (the workflow reads `secrets.IDIS_RECOVERY_READ_KEY` only in the `check` job's fetch step; nothing else may hold it — no repository- or organization-level copy). **Secure entry (owner only, never in chat):** generate a new key pair locally (`ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -N "" -C idis-ci-recovery-ro -f <private path>`); add the `.pub` half at the private repository → Settings → Deploy keys → Add deploy key, write access OFF; store the private half either through the GitHub UI (repository `albarami/IDIS` → Settings → Environments → `cir04-private-inputs` → Environment secrets → Add secret, name `IDIS_RECOVERY_READ_KEY`, value = the key file contents) or with `gh secret set IDIS_RECOVERY_READ_KEY --repo albarami/IDIS --env cir04-private-inputs < <private path>`; then delete or lock the local copy. Fable will verify only the secret's existence by name, never its value. **Qualification status (truthfully):** the documented condition is "P1–P4 must establish supported actual approval/job identity, token, rerun, custom-shell, cancellation, timeout and sink behavior before P5 real-key recovery" and "never provision a real recovery key before the required qualification". Complete: key-free mechanics reviewed (PASS_FOR_KEY_FREE_CUSTODY_SCOPE, then the C1–C5 corrections — literal-shell and supported-API checkpoints — reviewed and integrated; 296/307/323 checks without skips); hosted exact-run admission exercised for real on run 39014917801 attempt 2 (a genuine failed-jobs rerun, fresh external 25-field tuple, coordinator approval comment `idis-ci-approval-v1`, `admit` exit 0 under the environment), which evidences P1 (waiting gate and pre-approval identity), P2 (job-token read routes and comment round trip) and P3 (rerun freshness). **Not complete:** P4 — the temporary, separately reviewed synthetic sink/privacy probe inside the existing `check` job (synthetic seed and derived sentinel on both child streams, real exits 0/1/7, cancel-wait and step-timeout variants, private inspection of logs/annotations/summaries/artifacts) has not been executed ("Canary v3 is design-only"). Therefore the documented pre-real-key qualification is **not** complete for the intended candidate. Fable's recommendation: do not store the key yet; Fable prepares the P4 probe as a bounded reviewed temporary revision to be run on the next published exact head under the owner's specific approval, records the P1–P4 receipts, and only then asks for the key. **Owner decision (2026-10-17 ≈13:33 UTC, verbatim):** "Recovery credential: run the reviewed P4 synthetic sink/privacy probe on the next published exact head first; I will store IDIS_RECOVERY_READ_KEY only after its receipts are recorded." Consequence: no key is requested or stored until the P4 receipts exist; Fable prepares the P4 probe as a bounded, independently reviewed temporary revision inside the existing `check` job (serialized after J-03 because it touches `ci.yml`), publishes it with the combined checkpoint, prepares the exact-run approval for that one run and attempt, inspects the sinks privately, records the receipts, and then removes the temporary wiring under review before the key is stored. If the owner chooses to store the key before P4, the exposure to weigh is: a key that leaked through a public sink would grant read access to the ten private artifacts (four fund-derived policy/map files, five historical verification records, one public key) in that repository and nothing else; the fetch path itself fails closed on any pin mismatch.

**P4 execution plan (13:39 UTC, under the owner's P4-first ruling and the ≈13:35 UTC instruction "run P4 first using the reviewed canary procedure").** The reviewed procedure is the I-scope recipe `p1-p4-operational-recipe.md` with its M1–M7 amendment (local I evidence): a temporary, independently reviewed two-step probe inside the existing `check` job, immediately after checkout and before production admission — part A secret-free (real `observe`/approvals/`require_approval` with the job token, closed outputs: approval-comment length and SHA-256), part B receiving only the synthetic environment secret `IDIS_CI_CANARY_SEED` (derived sentinel emitted on both child streams, traceback, malformed JSON/XML, workflow-command lookalikes, runner file-command write attempts, real exits 0/1/7 through the production `capture_script`/`capture_environment` primitives, final exit 7 propagated to a failed job), finite literal variants V1 normal failure, V2 cancel-wait, V3 timeout-wait, each a separate reviewed PR #119 revision; after every attempt the coordinator privately downloads and scans every public sink (attempt/job logs, check-run annotations, rendered summaries, artifacts) for the seed and the sentinel without displaying matches; then the temporary wiring is removed under review and the seed revoked.
